```txt
│     ├── Server infrastructure (On-prem vs Cloud decision)     │
│     │   Options:                                            │
│     │   ├── Private Cloud (On-premise data center)          │
│     │   ├── Hybrid Cloud (Core on-prem, burst to cloud)     │
│     │   ├── Public Cloud (AWS/Azure/GCP - fully managed)    │
│     │   └── Recommendation: Usually hybrid for hospitals     │
│     │       (Keep sensitive data on-prem, use cloud for dev/│
│     │        non-critical workloads)                         │
│     ├── Network infrastructure (Bandwidth, redundancy)        │
│     ├── End-user devices (Computers, tablets, kiosks)        │
│     ├── Backup & DR strategy (RTO/RPO requirements)          │
│     └── Security infrastructure (Firewall, IDS/IPS, WAF)      │
│                                                             │
│  1.9 DATA MIGRATION STRATEGY                                │
│     Plan migration of existing data:                          │
│     ├── Patient master data (Cleanse, de-duplicate)          │
│     ├── Historical medical records (Digitize paper records)  │
│     ├── Financial history (Import trial balances)             │
│     ├── Employee data (Import from legacy HR system)         │
│     ├── Vendor/supplier data (Master data cleanup)           │
│     └── Inventory data (Stock take before go-live)           │
│                                                             │
│  1.10 VENDOR SELECTION (If buying vs building)              │
│     Evaluate options:                                        │
│     ├── Build Custom (In-house development team)             │
│     ├── Buy Commercial Product (Epic, Cerner, Meditech, etc.) │
│     ├── Buy + Customize (Product + local customization)       │
│     └── Open Source + Support (OpenMRS, OpenEMR, etc.)      │
│                                                             │
│     Decision Matrix:                                         │
│     ┌─────────────────┬──────────┬──────────┬──────────────┐ │
│     │ Factor           │ Build    │ Buy      │ Open Source │ │
│     ├─────────────────┼──────────┼──────────┼──────────────┤ │
│     │ Initial Cost     │ High     │ Very High│ Low         │ │
│     │ Time to Deploy   │ Long     │ Medium   │ Long        │ │
│     │ Customization    │ Full     │ Limited  │ Full        │ │
│     │ Ongoing Cost     │ Medium   │ High     │ Low-Medium  │ │
│     │ Control          │ Full     │ Limited  │ Full        │ │
│     │ Support          │ Internal │ Vendor   │ Community    │ │
│     │ Upgrade Path     │ Manual   │ Included│ Manual      │ │
│     └─────────────────┴──────────┴──────────┴──────────────┘ │
│                                                             │
│  1.11 PROJECT PLAN FINALIZATION                             │
│     Deliverables:                                             │
│     ├── Detailed project plan (Gantt chart, MS Project)      │
│     ├── Resource plan (FTEs, skills, timeline)               │
│     ├── Budget estimate (CAPEX + OPEX for 3-5 years)        │
│     ├── Communication plan (Stakeholder engagement)           │
│     ├── Risk management plan (Mitigation strategies)          │
│     ├── Change management plan (Adoption approach)            │
│     ├── Training plan (By role, by phase)                   │
│     ├── Testing strategy (UAT, integration, performance)      │
│     ├── Go-live cutover plan (Parallel vs Big Bang)          │
│     └── Post-go-live support plan (Hypercare period)          │
│                                                             │
│  1.12 BASELINE METRICS CAPTURE                               │
│     Measure current performance BEFORE implementation:          │
│     ├── Average patient wait times (By department)             │
│     ├── Daily patient volumes (OPD/IPD/ER)                    │
│     ├── Revenue cycle time (Days from service to payment)     │
│     ├── Billing error rate (Claim rejection %)                │
│     ├── Inventory turnover rate                               │
│     ├── Staff productivity metrics                           │
│     ├── Patient satisfaction scores (Baseline NPS)            │
│     └── IT system uptime/downtime statistics                 │
│                                                             │
│     (These will be compared against post-implementation       │
│      metrics to measure ROI and success)                     │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

PHASE 0 DELIVERABLES CHECKLIST:
□ Project Charter Document (Signed)
□ Stakeholder Register (With RACI matrix)
□ As-Is Process Documentation Pack
□ Gap Analysis Report
□ Requirements Specification (Prioritized)
□ Technical Architecture Decision Document
□ Infrastructure Plan & Diagrams
□ Data Migration Strategy Document
□ Vendor Evaluation Report (If applicable)
□ Detailed Project Plan (MS Project/Gantt)
□ Budget Proposal (Approved)
□ Risk Register (Updated)
□ Baseline Metrics Report
□ Phase 1 Readiness Assessment
```

## PHASE 1: CORE SYSTEM (PATIENT + APPOINTMENT + DOCTOR)

**Duration: 3 Months | Team: 8-12 Members**

### **Objective**

Build the foundational modules that all other modules depend upon - without patients, appointments, and doctors defined, nothing else works.

### **Scope**

```txt
MODULES IN THIS PHASE:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                             │
│  ✅ MODULE 1: PATIENT MANAGEMENT (Full)                     │
│     ├── Patient Registration (New & Returning)               │
│     ├── Demographics Management                             │
│     ├── Family/Household Linking                            │
│     ├── Identification Documents                            │
│     ├── Insurance Enrollment                                │
│     ├── Patient Categories & Pricing                        │
│     ├── Patient History Dashboard                           │
│     ├── Duplicate Detection/Merge                          │
│     └── Basic Search & Listing                              │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ MODULE 2: APPOINTMENT & SCHEDULING (Core)               │
│     ├── Appointment Types Configuration                      │
│     ├── Doctor Schedule Management                           │
│     ├── Slot Management (Fixed, Wave, Open)                  │
│     ├── Multi-channel Booking (Reception, Kiosk, Phone)     │
│     ├── Appointment Workflow States                        │
│     ├── Basic Reminder System (SMS/Email)                   │
│     ├── Token/Queue Number Generation                       │
│     ├── Calendar Views (Doctor, Department, Resource)        │
│     └── Basic Availability Checking                         │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ MODULE 11: DOCTOR MANAGEMENT (Basic Profile)            │
│     ├── Doctor Master Registration                          │
│     ├── Qualifications & Credentials (Basic)                │
│     ├── Department & Specialty Assignment                   │
│     ├── Schedule Template Definition                        │
│     ├── Basic Availability Setup                            │
│     ├── Consultation Room Assignment                       │
│     └── Doctor Directory/Search                            │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ MODULE 16: USER & ROLE MANAGEMENT (Foundation)          │
│     ├── User Registration (Staff accounts)                   │
│     ├── Role Definition (Admin, Doctor, Nurse, etc.)        │
│     ├── Basic Permission Assignment                         │
│     ├── Authentication Setup (Login/password)                │
│     ├── Session Management                                 │
│     └── Basic Audit Logging (Login/logout/actions)          │
│                                                             │
│  ⚠️ PARTIAL: MODULE 17: NOTIFICATION (Basic)               │

│  ├── Doctors table & related                                │
│  ├── Users, Roles, Permissions tables                      │
│  ├── Departments table                                      │
│  ├── Schedules table                                       │
│  └── Basic audit logs table                                │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

```txt
SPRINT BREAKDOWN (2-week sprints):

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                                                             │
│  PILOT LAUNCH (Week 13 - Soft Launch):                      │

│  ├── Intensive support during pilot (Help desk on standby)    │
│  ├── Daily standup meetings to address issues immediately     │
│  ├── Collect feedback rapidly, iterate quickly               │
│  └── Pilot success criteria defined upfront                  │
│      (e.g., 95% registrations in new system, <5% errors)    │

│  TRAINING (Conducted during Weeks 10-13):                   │

│  ├── End-user training (Role-based sessions):                │
│  │   ├── Front Desk Staff: 8 hours (Registration focus)     │
│  │   ├── Schedulers: 8 hours (Appointment focus)            │
│  │   ├── Doctors: 4 hours (Schedule/viewing focus)          │
│  │   └── IT Admin: 16 hours (System administration)          │
│  ├── Quick reference guides (Laminated cheat sheets)         │
│  ├── Video tutorials (For self-paced learning)               │
│  └── Sandbox practice environment (Safe place to practice)  │
│                                                             │
│  DATA MIGRATION (Phase 1 Data Only):                        │
│  ├── Extract patient master from legacy system               │
│  ├── Cleanse data (Fix formats, remove duplicates)           │
│  ├── Map fields to new system structure                     │
│  ├── Transform and load into new system                     │
│  ├── Verify counts match (Legacy count = New system count)    │
│  └── Validate sample records for accuracy                   │
│                                                             │
│  CUT-OVER DECISION (End of Week 13/14):                    │
│  ├── Go/No-Go Checklist Review:                             │
│  │   ✓ All critical bugs resolved                           │
│  │   ✓ Pilot success criteria met                           │
│  │   ✓ Training completion >90%                             │
│  │   ✓ Data migration validated                             │
│  │   ✓ Support team ready (24/7 for first week)             │
│  │   ✓ Rollback plan tested and ready                      │
│  │   ✓ Communication to all stakeholders sent               │
│  └── Decision: Proceed to full go-live OR extend pilot      │
│                                                             │
│  PHASE 1 COMPLETE! 🎉                                        │
│  Foundation established for all subsequent phases            │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## PHASE 2: BILLING & FINANCE

```txt
ACCELERATION OPTIONS:
• Double team size → Halve duration (~1.5-2 years)
• Parallel track development (Multiple streams) → Faster
• Buy commercial product instead of build → Reduce Phase 1-10 significantly
• Cloud-hosted SaaS → Reduce infrastructure setup time
• Phased rollout (One branch at a time) → Manage risk better
```

# 🧱 PART 4: MODULE-WISE DATABASE DESIGN

## **Complete Table Structures with Relationships**

### **Design Philosophy Applied Here**

```txt
Every table follows these principles:
✅ Primary Key (PK): Unique identifier (Auto-increment INT or GUID)
✅ Foreign Keys (FK): Referential integrity to parent tables
✅ Audit Fields: CreatedAt, UpdatedAt, CreatedBy, UpdatedBy
✅ Soft Delete: IsDeleted flag (Never hard-delete important data)
✅ Status Fields: Enum/Status columns for state tracking
✅ TenantId/BranchId: For multi-tenancy support
✅ Indexes: On frequently queried columns (FKs, search fields, dates)
✅ Constraints: NOT NULL where appropriate, CHECK constraints for valid data
```

# PART 5: DESIGN PRINCIPLES

## **Enterprise Architecture Standards Applied**

### **1. Database Design Principles (Applied Throughout)**

```txt
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              DATABASE DESIGN PRINCIPLES                       │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 1. NORMALIZATION (3NF Minimum)                          │
│     Eliminate data redundancy while balancing read performance│
│     - First Normal Form: Atomic values, no repeating groups  │
│     - Second Normal Form: No partial dependencies on PK      │
│     - Third Normal Form: No transitive dependencies         │
│     Exception: Denormalize intentionally for read-heavy      │
│     reporting tables (Data Warehouse uses Star Schema)       │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 2. REFERENTIAL INTEGRITY                                │
│     Every foreign key properly defined with:                 │
│     - ON UPDATE CASCADE (Parent update flows to children)    │
│     - ON DELETE RESTRICT (Prevent orphan records)            │
│     - Or ON DELETE SET NULL (Soft removal of references)     │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 3. CONSISTENT NAMING CONVENTIONS                        │
│     PascalCase for table names: Patients, UserRoles          │
│     camelCase for column names: PatientId, CreatedAt         │
│     Prefix FK columns with referenced table name:           │
│       DoctorId → references Users, DepartmentId → Depts      │
│     Boolean prefixes: Is, Has, Can, Should, IsDeleted       │
│     Timestamp suffixes: At (CreatedAt, UpdatedAt)           │
│     Status/Type suffixes: _Status, _Type, _Id             │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 4. AUDIT FIELDS ON EVERY TABLE                          │
│     CreatedAt DATETIME -- When record was born               │
│     UpdatedAt DATETIME -- Last modification timestamp        │
│     CreatedBy BIGINT → Users.UserId -- Creator identity     │
│     UpdatedBy BIGINT → Users.UserId -- Last modifier        │
│     These four fields appear on EVERY single table          │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 5. SOFT DELETE PATTERN                                  │
│     IsDeleted BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE on every table          │
│     Queries include: WHERE IsDeleted = 0 (or IS NULL)       │
│     Hard deletes only for: Temp tables, Logs, Cache         │
│     Actual deletion runs as scheduled job (purge old data)   │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 6. STATUS/STATE FIELDS                                  │
│     Use ENUM for known states (not free text)               │
│     Examples: Status ENUM('Active','Inactive','Pending'...)   │
│     Benefits: Type safety, indexable, self-documenting      │
│     Include status change tracking (PreviousStatus field)   │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 7. TENANT & BRANCH ISOLATION                            │
│     TenantId BIGINT on ALL tables (Multi-tenant support)      │
│     BranchId BIGINT on operational tables                   │
│     Row-Level Security (RLS) policies enforce data isolation│
│     Every query filtered by tenant context automatically      │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 8. INDEXING STRATEGY                                   │
│     Index all Foreign Keys (Standard practice)               │
│     Index frequently searched columns (Name, Code, Date)     │
│     Composite indexes for common query patterns               │
│     Avoid over-indexing (Slows writes, uses storage)        │
│     Use covering indexes where appropriate                   │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 9. DATA TYPE BEST PRACTICES                             │
│     Money: DECIMAL(12,2) or DECIMAL(18,4) -- NEVER Float   │
│     Percentages: DECIMAL(5,2) or DECIMAL(5,4)              │
│     Dates/Times: Always DATETIME or DATE (Not VARCHAR)      │
│     Booleans: TINYINT(1) or BOOLEAN (Not 0/1 INT)          │
│     Large Text: TEXT (Not VARCHAR(4000+) unless needed)      │
│     JSON: Use JSON type for flexible schema data            │
│     Binary: BLOB for images/files (or store path preference)│
│     Phone: VARCHAR(20) -- Include country code possibility  │
│     Email: VARCHAR(200) -- RFC compliant length             │
│                                                             │
│  ✅ 10. CONSTRAINTS & VALIDATION                            │
│     NOT NULL on mandatory fields                            │
│     UNIQUE on natural keys (Email, Username, MRN)           │
│     CHECK constraints for business rules (Age >= 0, etc.)   │
│     DEFAULT values for common cases (CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, 0) │
│     ENUM for categorical data (Limited choices)             │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### **2. API Design Principles**

```txt
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              RESTful API DESIGN STANDARDS                    │
│                                                             │
│  URL CONVENTIONS:                                           │
│  Base URL: https://api.hospital.com/v1/                    │
│  Resources: Plural nouns (/patients, /appointments)          │
│  Actions: Verbs, not URLs (POST to create, not /createPt)  │
│  Nesting: Logical hierarchy (/patients/{id}/appointments)    │
│  Filtering: Query parameters (?status=active&dept=cardio)   │
│  Pagination: ?page=1&limit=20&sort=-createdAt             │
│  Search: /search?q=term&fields=name,phone                   │
│                                                             │
│  HTTP METHODS:                                              │
│  GET    → Retrieve (List or Single)                         │
│  POST   → Create (New resource)                             │
│  PUT    → Full Update (Replace entire resource)             │
│  PATCH  → Partial Update (Only provided fields)             │
│  DELETE → Soft Delete (Set IsDeleted=true)                  │
│                                                             │
│  RESPONSE FORMAT:                                           │
│  Success:                                                 │
│  {                                                        │
│    "success": true,                                       │
│    "data": { ... }, // The requested resource(s)          │
│    "message": null,                                      │
│    "pagination": { // Only for lists                       │
│      "page": 1,                                          │
│      "limit": 20,                                        │
│      "totalRecords": 1500,                               │
│      "totalPages": 75                                    │
│    }                                                       │
│  }                                                        │
│                                                             │
│  Error:                                                   │
│  {                                                        │
│    "success": false,                                      │
│    "data": null,                                         │
│    "error": {                                             │
│      "code": "VALIDATION_ERROR",                          │
│      "message": "Invalid email format",                    │
│      "details": [ // Array of field-level errors          │
│        {                                                  │
│          "field": "email",                               │
│          "message": "Must be valid email address"         │
│        }                                                  │
│      ]                                                   │
│    },                                                     │
│    "traceId": "abc-123-def-456" // For debugging         │
│  }                                                        │
│                                                             │
│  VERSIONING:                                               │
│  URL versioning: /v1/, /v2/ (Current version)             │
│  Header versioning: Accept-Version: v2 (Alternative)       │
│  Backward compatibility maintained for at least 1 version   │
│                                                             │
│  AUTHENTICATION:                                           │
│  Header: Authorization: Bearer <jwt_token>                 │
│  JWT Payload: { userId, roleId, tenantId, exp, iat }     │
│  Token refresh: /auth/refresh with refresh_token           │
│                                                             │
│  RATE LIMITING:                                             │
│  Per-user: 100 requests/minute                           │
│  Per-IP: 1000 requests/minute                            │
│  Headers returned: X-RateLimit-Limit, X-RateLimit-Remaining│
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### **3. Security Architecture Principles**

```txt
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              SECURITY-IN-DEPTH ARCHITECTURE                  │
│                                                             │
│  LAYER 1: NETWORK SECURITY                                 │
│  ├── Web Application Firewall (WAF)                        │
│  ├── DDoS Protection                                       │
│  ├── SSL/TLS 1.3+ (HTTPS everywhere)                       │
│  ├── Network Segmentation (DMZ for public-facing servers)   │
│  ├── Intrusion Detection/Prevention System (IDS/IPS)        │
│  └── VPN for administrative access                          │
│                                                             │
│  LAYER 2: APPLICATION SECURITY                              │
│  ├── Input Validation (All user inputs sanitized)           │
│  ├── Output Encoding (XSS prevention)                       │
│  ├── SQL Injection Prevention (Parameterized queries ONLY)  │
│  ├── CSRF Tokens (State-changing forms)                    │
│  ├── CORS Configuration (Whitelist origins)                  │
│  ├── HTTP Strict Transport Security headers                 │
│  ├── Content Security Policy headers                        │
│  └── Rate Limiting (Brute force protection)                 │
│                                                             │
│  LAYER 3: AUTHENTICATION &
```

# **HOSPITAL FULL ERP SYSTEM - COMPLETE ENTERPRISE DESIGN** (Continued)

```txt
│  LAYER 3: AUTHENTICATION & AUTHORIZATION                   │
│  ├── Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA)                       │
│  │   ├── Something you know: Password/PIN                  │
│  │   ├── Something you have: Phone/Token/Smart card        │
│  │   └── Something you are: Biometrics                    │
│  ├── JWT (JSON Web Tokens) for stateless auth              │
│  │   ├── Access token: Short-lived (15-30 min)            │
│  │   ├── Refresh token: Longer-lived (7 days)             │
│  │   └── Stored securely (HttpOnly cookie, not localStorage)│
│  ├── Session Management                                    │
│  │   ├── Absolute timeout: Max session duration (8 hours)    │
│  │   ├── Idle timeout: Auto-logout after inactivity (15 min)│
│  │   ├── Concurrent sessions: Limit per user (max 3)         │
│  │   └── Session fixation prevention                        │
│  ├── Password Policies                                      │
│  │   ├── Minimum length: 12 characters                      │
│  │   ├── Complexity: Upper+lower+number+special required   │
│  │   ├── History: Cannot reuse last 12 passwords           │
│  │   ├── Expiry: Force change every 90 days                │
│  │   └── Breach detection: Force change if compromise      │
│  │       suspected                                          │
│  └── Account Lockout                                        │
│      ├── Lock after 5 failed attempts                       │
│      ├── Progressive delay: 30s, 60s, 120s between attempts  │
│      ├── Auto-unlock after 30 minutes                       │
│      └── Admin unlock capability                             │
│                                                             │
│  LAYER 4: AUTHORIZATION (RBAC + ABAC)                       │
│  ├── Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)                       │
│  │   ├── Roles define permissions (Group-based)             │
│  │   ├── Hierarchical roles (Admin > Manager > User)        │
│  │   ├── Principle of Least Privilege (Minimum needed)       │
│  │   └── Separation of Duties (No single person can do all) │
│  ├── Attribute-Based Access Control (ABAC)                 │
│  │   ├── Context-aware decisions (Time, location, device)   │
│  │   ├── Data-level security (Row-level: Can only see own   │
│  │   │   department patients)                                │
│  │   └── Fine-grained permissions (CRUD per resource type)  │
│  ├── Break-Glass / Emergency Access                         │
│  │   ├── Override normal restrictions in emergency          │
│  │   ├── Requires MFA re-authentication                    │
│  │   ├── Full audit logging of break-glass events          │
│  │   └── Mandatory post-incident review                     │
│  └── Privileged Access Management (PAM)                    │
│      ├── Admin accounts: Separate credentials, extra MFA     │
│      ├── Just-in-time access: Temporary elevated privs       │
│      ├── Session recording: Screen capture for admin sessions│
│      └── Regular access reviews (Quarterly certification)      │
│                                                             │
│  LAYER 5: DATA PROTECTION                                   │
│  ├── Encryption at Rest                                     │
│  │   ├── AES-256 encryption for sensitive fields            │
│  │   ├── Database-level encryption (TDE)                    │
│  │   ├── File system encryption (BitLocker/LUKS)           │
│  │   └── Backup encryption (Encrypted backup media)          │
│  ├── Encryption in Transit                                  │
│  │   ├── TLS 1.3 for all communications                   │
│  │   ├── Certificate pinning (Prevent MITM)                 │
│  │   ├── HSTS headers enforced                              │
│  │   └── Internal traffic also encrypted                    │
│  ├── Data Masking                                           │
│  │   ├── Dynamic masking: Show last 4 digits of SSN        │
│  │   ├── Static masking: Hash values stored, original never  │
│  │   │   retrievable in plain text                           │
│  │   ├── Role-based masking: Doctors see full, Front desk   │
│  │   │   sees masked                                         │
│  │   └── Audit trail shows who accessed unmasked data       │
│  ├── Key Management                                        │
│  │   ├── Hardware Security Module (HSM) for key storage     │
│  │   ├── Key rotation policy (Annual or upon compromise)     │
│  │   ├── Split knowledge (Multiple people hold key parts)   │
│  │   └── Secure key distribution channels                  │
│  └── Data Classification & Handling                          │
│      ├── Labels: Public, Internal, Confidential, Restricted  │
│      ├── DLP (Data Loss Prevention) rules based on class     │
│      ├── Automatic classification using ML                    │
│      └── Retention/deletion based on classification          │
│                                                             │
│  LAYER 6: MONITORING & AUDIT                               │
│  ├── SIEM (Security Information & Event Management)          │
│  │   ├── Centralized log aggregation from all systems       │
│  │   ├── Real-time correlation of security events            │
│  │   ├── Automated alerting on suspicious patterns          │
│  │   └── Compliance reporting (PCI-DSS, HIPAA, GDPR)       │
│  ├── Vulnerability Management                               │
│  │   ├── Regular scanning (Weekly automated vulnerability   │
│  │   │   scans)                                             │
│  │   ├── Penetration testing (Quarterly by external firm)    │
│  │   ├── Patch management (Within 30 days of patch release) │
│  │   └── CVE tracking for all components                   │
│  ├── Security Analytics                                     │
│  │   ├── UEBA (User and Entity Behavior Analytics)          │
│  │   ├── Anomaly detection (Unusual access patterns)         │
│  │   ├── Threat intelligence feeds (Known bad IPs/domains)  │
│  │   └── Risk scoring for users, assets, activities       │
│  └── Incident Response                                     │
│      ├── Playbooks for common incident types                │
│      ├── Forensic investigation capabilities                 │
│      ├── Evidence preservation chain                       │
│      └── Post-incident review and improvement               │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### **4. Performance & Scalability Principles**

```txt
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│           PERFORMANCE & SCALABILITY STANDARDS                 │
│                                                             │
│  DATABASE PERFORMANCE:                                       │
│  ├── Query Optimization                                      │
│  │   ├── EXPLAIN analyze on all slow queries (>100ms)       │
│  │   ├── Proper indexing strategy (Covering indexes)         │
│  │   ├── Avoid SELECT * (Specify needed columns only)        │
│  │   ├── Avoid N+1 queries (Use JOINs or batch loading)     │
│  │   ├── Pagination mandatory for list queries               │
│  │   └── Read replicas for read-heavy workloads             │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Connection Pooling                                     │
│  │   ├── Reuse connections (Don't open/close per query)     │
│  │   ├── Optimal pool size (CPU cores * 2 + 1 formula)     │
│  │   ├── Timeout settings (Query timeout, connection timeout)│
│  │   └── Health checks (Remove dead connections)             │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Caching Strategy                                        │
│  │   ├── L1 Cache: In-memory cache within application      │
│  │   │   (Redis/Memcached) - Session data, frequent lookups │
│  │   ├── L2 Cache: Distributed cache cluster               │
│  │   │   (Redis Cluster) - Shared data across instances     │
│  │   ├── L3 Cache: CDN/Edge cache (Static assets)          │
│  │   ├── Cache invalidation: Event-driven updates            │
│  │   │   (Update → Invalidate cache → Next request refreshes)│
│  │   └── Cache-aside pattern (Populate cache on miss)      │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Database Partitioning                                   │
│  │   ├── Horizontal partitioning (Range/List/Hash)         │
│  │   │   Example: Patients table partitioned by year        │
│  │   ├── Vertical partitioning (Split wide tables)          │
│  │   │   Example: Patient demographics separate from clinical│
│  │   ├── Archive old data (Cold storage for historical)     │
│  │   └── Partition pruning (Queries skip irrelevant parts)  │
│                                                             │
│  APPLICATION PERFORMANCE:                                    │
│  ├── Response Time Targets (SLAs)                            │
│  │   ├── API P95 response time < 500ms                      │
│  │   ├── Page load time < 2 seconds (Above fold)           │
│  │   ├── Critical operations < 200ms (Login, save)          │
│  │   └── Background/batch operations async (No user wait)   │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Asynchronous Processing                                 │
│  │   ├── Message queues for long-running tasks              │
│  │   │   (RabbitMQ/Apache Kafka/AWS SQS)                   │
│  │   ├── Event-driven architecture (Fire & forget)          │
│  │   ├── Background job processing (Report generation)      │
│  │   └── WebSockets for real-time updates (No polling)     │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Frontend Optimization                                  │
│  │   ├── Code splitting (Lazy load routes)                  │
│  │   ├── Image optimization (WebP format, lazy loading)      │
│  │   ├── Minification & Bundling (Reduce payload size)     │
│  │   ├── CDN delivery (Static assets from edge locations)   │
│  │   ├── Service Worker caching (Offline capability)        │
│  │   └── Tree shaking (Remove unused code)                  │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Resource Optimization                                   │
│      ├── Compression (Gzip/Brotli for responses)             │
│      ├── Pagination (Never load unlimited records)            │
│      ├── Field selection (APIs allow specifying needed fields)│
│      ├── Rate limiting (Prevent abuse, protect resources)     │
│      └── Efficient serialization (Protocol Buffers vs JSON    │
│          for internal microservices)                          │
│                                                             │
│  SCALABILITY PATTERNS:                                       │
│  ├── Horizontal Scaling (Scale Out)                          │
│  │   ├── Stateless application servers (Add more behind LB)│
│  │   ├── Load balancer (Round-robin, least connections)     │
│  │   ├── Database read replicas (Scale reads independently)  │
│  │   ├── Microservices decomposition (Independent scaling)   │
│  │   └── Container orchestration (Kubernetes auto-scaling) │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Vertical Scaling (Scale Up)                             │
│  │   ├── Upgrade server CPU/RAM when hitting limits        │
│  │   ├── Database server upgrades (More powerful hardware)   │
│  │   ├── SSD storage (Faster I/O than HDD)                 │
│  │   └── In-memory databases for ultra-fast access           │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Database Scaling Strategies                             │
│  │   ├── Read-Write Splitting (Writes to master, reads to   │
│  │   │   replica)                                            │
│  │   ├── Sharding (Split data across multiple DB servers)   │
│  │   │   - Range sharding: Users A-M on DB1, N-Z on DB2    │
│  │   │   - Hash sharding: Consistent hash distribution     │
│  │   ├── Federation (Different data in different databases,  │
│  │   │   unified via API layer)                             │
│  │   └── CQRS (Command Query Responsibility Segregation)     │
│  │       - Write-optimized store (Normalized SQL DB)        │
│  │       - Read-optimized store (Denormalized NoSQL/Search) │
│  │                                                           │
│  CAPACITY PLANNING:                                          │
│  ├── Load Testing (Before go-live)                           │
│  │   ├── Simulate expected concurrent users                  │
│  │   ├── Identify breaking points                            │
│  │   ├── Plan capacity for growth (2x headroom)            │
│  │   └── Tools: JMeter, Gatling, Locust, k6               │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Monitoring & Alerting                                   │
│  │   ├── APM (Application Performance Monitoring)             │
│  │   │   (New Relic, Datadog, AppDynamics)                 │
│  │   ├── Infrastructure monitoring (Prometheus + Grafana)     │
│  │   ├── Database performance monitoring (Slow query log)    │
│  │   ├── Error rate tracking (Sentry, Rollbar)               │
│  │   └── Auto-scaling triggers (Based on CPU/memory metrics) │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Degradation Strategies (When under heavy load)          │
│      ├── Graceful degradation (Show partial data if full data  │
│      │   slow)                                               │
│      ├── Queue-based processing (Queue requests, process as   │
│      │   able)                                                │
│      ├── Circuit breaker (Stop calling failing services, show  │
│      │   cached/fallback data)                                │
│      ├── Feature flags (Disable non-critical features to reduce  │
│      │   load)                                                │
│      └── Static content serving (During outages, serve cached  │
│          pages)                                              │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### **5. Integration Architecture Principles**
```txt
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│            INTEGRATION ARCHITECTURE STANDARDS                 │
│                                                             │
│  INTEGRATION PHILOSOPHY:                                    │
│  ├── API-First Design (All integrations via APIs)            │
│  │   ├── Well-documented API contracts (OpenAPI/Swagger)    │
│  │   ├── Versioned APIs (/v1/, /v2/)                       │
│  │   ├── Sandbox/UAT environment for partner testing        │
│  │   └── Developer portal with interactive documentation     │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Loose Coupling                                         │
│  │   ├── Systems communicate via well-defined interfaces     │
│  │   ├── No direct database access between systems           │
│  │   ├── Message queues for async communication              │
│  │   └── Each system can be updated independently           │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Failure Isolation                                       │
│  │   ├── One system's failure shouldn't cascade              │
│  │   ├── Circuit breakers prevent cascading failures         │
│  │   ├── Retry with exponential backoff                     │
│  │   └── Fallback/default responses when downstream fails   │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Idempotency                                             │
│      ├── Same request processed twice = Same result          │
│      ├── Use unique request IDs for deduplication            │
│      └── Safe to retry without side effects                  │
│                                                             │
│  HEALTHCARE-SPECIFIC INTEROPERABILITY STANDARDS:             │
│  ├── HL7 Version 2.x (Legacy Clinical Systems)                │
│  │   ├── Message-based event-triggered communication          │
│  │   ├── Segments: PID (Patient ID), OBR (Order), OBX (Obs)│
│  │   ├── Common triggers: ADT (Admit/Discharge/Transfer),     │
│  │   │   ORM (Pharmacy), ORU (Results)                      │
│  │   └── Still dominant in lab/radiology interfaces          │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── HL7 FHIR R4 (Modern Interoperability Standard)           │
│  │   ├── RESTful JSON-based resources                      │
│  │   ├── Resources: Patient, Observation, MedicationOrder,  │
│  │   │   DiagnosticReport, Encounter, Practitioner          │
│  │   ├── Preferred for new integrations (Patient portals,   │
│  │   │   health apps, government systems)                    │
│  │   └── Growing adoption globally                          │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── DICOM (Medical Imaging)                                  │
│  │   ├── For radiology images (X-ray, CT, MRI, Ultrasound) │
│  │   ├── Objects: Images, Studies, Series, Instances       │
│  │   ├── Services: C-STORE (Storage), C-FIND (Query),       │
│  │   │   C-MOVE (Send), C-GET (Retrieve), WADO (Web)       │
│  │   └── PACS integration for image archival/retrieval       │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── ICD-10 / ICD-11 (Diagnosis Coding)                     │
│  │   ├── Standard diagnosis codes worldwide                  │
│  │   ├── Required for billing, epidemiology, quality        │
│  │   ├── Structured coding enables analytics                │
│  │   └── Mapped to SNOMED CT for clinical granularity       │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── LOINC (Lab Test Codes)                                   │
│  │   ├── Universal identifiers for laboratory tests          │
│  │   ├── Enables semantic interoperability of lab results   │
│  │   └── Essential for LIS integration standardization      │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── EDI X12 (Insurance Claims)                               │
│      ├── 270/271 Eligibility inquiry/response               │
│      ├── 837 Healthcare claim (Professional/institutional)    │
│      ├── 835 Remittance advice (Payment explanation)         │
│      └── 276 Claim status request/response                   │
│                                                             │
│  INTEGRATION PATTERNS:                                       │
│  Pattern 1: Synchronous Request-Response (Real-time needs)  │
│  ├── Use case: Insurance eligibility check                  │
│  ├── Protocol: REST API / SOAP Web Service                  │
│  ├── Timeout: Must respond within seconds                  │
│  └── Example: ERP calls TPA API → Waits for response → Shows │
│          result                                                  │
│                                                             │
│  Pattern 2: Asynchronous Fire-and-Forget (Notifications)    │
│  ├── Use case: Send SMS/email notification                 │
│  ├── Protocol: Message Queue (RabbitMQ/SQS) or webhook POST   │
│  ├── No immediate response needed                             │
│  └── Example: Lab result ready → Push notification to patient │
│          portal → Don't wait for patient to read it           │
│                                                             │
│  Pattern 3: Event-Driven Pub/Sub (Decoupled systems)        │
│  ├── Use case: Cross-module updates (Patient admitted → Notify │
│  │   pharmacy, dietary, billing simultaneously)               │
│  ├── Protocol: Apache Kafka / Redis Streams / RabbitMQ        │
│  ├── Publisher doesn't know who consumes                    │
│  └── Example: Admission service publishes 'PatientAdmitted'     │
│          event → Pharmacy, Billing, Nursing services subscribe │
│          and act independently                                 │
│                                                             │
│  Pattern 4: Batch/Scheduled (Bulk data exchange)             │
│  ├── Use case: Government reporting, claims submission        │
│  ├── Protocol: File transfer (SFTP/FTP) or batch API calls    │
│  ├── Runs at scheduled times (Daily, Weekly)                 │
│  └── Example: Send daily disease surveillance report to health │
│          department at midnight                                  │
│                                                             │
│  Pattern 5: File-Based (Legacy integration)                   │
│  ├── Use case: Receiving documents from external entities     │
│  ├── Protocol: SFTP drop folders, Email parsing, OCR          │
│  ├── Less preferred but sometimes necessary                  │
│  └── Example: Receive lab results PDF from external lab via    │
│          email attachment → Parse and import into system       │
│                                                             │
│  ERROR HANDLING IN INTEGRATIONS:                             │
│  ├── Retry strategies (Transient failures)                    │
│  │   ├── Immediate retry (Once, for network blips)           │
│  │   ├── Fixed interval retry (Every 1 min, 3 times)        │
│  │   ├── Exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s, 8s... max 5 min)  │
│  │   └── Jitter (Add randomness to avoid thundering herd)     │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Dead Letter Queues (Failed messages)                    │
│  │   ├── Messages that failed after max retries              │
│  │   ├── Stored for manual inspection and replay           │
│  │   ├── Alert admin when message enters DLQ                │
│  │   └── Never lose messages (Guaranteed delivery)           │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Circuit Breaker (Prevent cascading failures)            │
│  │   ├── CLOSED: Requests pass through normally              │
│  │   ├── OPEN: After failure threshold → Block requests fast │
│  │   │   (Return fallback/cached data immediately)           │
│  │   └── HALF_OPEN: Periodic test requests to see if recovered│
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Compensation Transactions (Undo effects of failed steps)  │
│      If Step 3 fails after Steps 1 & 2 succeeded:            │
│      → Execute compensating action to undo Steps 1 & 2         │
│      → Maintain data consistency across systems                │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### **6. Code Quality & Development Standards**

```txt
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│            CODE QUALITY & DEVELOPMENT STANDARDS               │
│                                                             │
│  VERSION CONTROL:                                             │
│  ├── Git workflow (GitFlow or Trunk-Based)                   │
│  │   ├── main branch: Production-ready code only             │
│  │   ├── develop branch: Integration branch                  │
│  │   ├── feature branches: One feature per branch            │
│  │   ├── hotfix branches: Emergency fixes to production      │
│  │   └── release branches: Preparation for deployment        │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Commit message conventions                              │
│  │   ├── Type: feat/fix/docs/refactor/style/test/chore      │
│  │   ├── Scope: Brief description of what changed            │
│  │   ├── Issue tracker reference: #JIRA-123                   │
│  │   └── Example: feat(PHASE-2): Add insurance eligibility   │
│  │       check endpoint #TICKET-456                            │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Branch protection rules (Direct push to main forbidden) │
│                                                             │
│  CODE REVIEW STANDARDS:                                      │
│  ├── Every PR must be reviewed by at least 1 peer             │
│  ├── Size limit: Maximum 400 lines changed per PR            │
│  ├── No merge conflicts allowed (Resolver must update first)   │
│  ├── All tests must pass (Unit + Integration)                │
│  ├── Code coverage minimum: 80% for new code, 70% for modified │
│  ├── Security scan must pass (No critical/high vulnerabilities)│
│  ├── Documentation required for complex logic                  │
│  └── Approval from tech lead for significant changes          │
│                                                             │
│  CODING STANDARDS (Language-Agnostic Principles):             │
│  ├── Meaningful Names (Variables, Functions, Classes)         │
│  │   ✅ patientCount, calculateDosage(), UserService         │
│  │   ❌ pc, calc(), us, svc                                   │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Single Responsibility (One function = one task)          │
│  │   ✅ validatePatientEligibility()                        │
│  │   ❌ processAndValidateAndSaveAndNotify()                 │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── DRY Principle (Don't Repeat Yourself)                   │
│  │   Extract common logic into shared utilities               │
│  │   Use inheritance/composition appropriately                │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── KISS Principle (Keep It Simple, Stupid)                 │
│  │   Simple solutions preferred over clever ones             │
│  │   Avoid premature optimization                          │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── YAGNI (You Aren't Gonna Need It)                       │
│  │   Don't build flexibility you don't need yet              │
│  │   Build for current requirements, refactor when needed     │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── SOLID Principles (Object-Oriented Design)                │
│  │   S: Single Responsibility                               │
│  │   O: Open/Closed (Open for extension, closed for mod)    │
│  │   L: Liskov Substitution (Subtypes must be substitutable) │
│  │   I: Interface Segregation (Many specific interfaces)      │
│  │   D: Dependency Inversion (Depend on abstractions)        │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Clean Code Practices                                    │
│  │   ├── Function length: Max 20-30 lines                    │
│  │   ├── File length: Max 300-500 lines                     │
│  │   ├── Nesting depth: Max 3-4 levels                       │
│  │   ├── Parameters: Max 3-5 per function                   │
│  │   ├── Magic numbers: Named constants only                  │
│  │   ├── Comments: Explain WHY, not WHAT (Code is self-      │
│  │   │   documenting)                                          │
│  │   └── Dead code: Remove unused variables/functions         │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Error Handling                                          │
│      ├── Custom exception classes (Not generic Exception)      │
│      ├── Specific error codes (ERR_PATIENT_NOT_FOUND, etc.)  │
│      ├── Wrap low-level exceptions at boundaries              │
│      ├── Log exceptions with full context (Stack trace, params)│
│      └── Never swallow exceptions silently (At minimum log)    │
│                                                             │
│  TESTING STANDARDS:                                          │
│  ├── Unit Tests (Developer-written)                           │
│  │   ├── Test individual functions/methods in isolation      │
│  │   ├── Mock external dependencies (DB, APIs, services)      │
│  │   ├── Cover happy path + edge cases + error conditions    │
│  │   ├── Fast execution (Unit test suite < 5 minutes)         │
│  │   └── Tools: Jest/JUnit/PyTest, Mockito/Unittest         │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Integration Tests                                       │
│  │   ├── Test component interactions (API + Database)         │
│  │   ├── Test with real database (Test container/Docker)      │
│  │   ├── Test API contracts (Request/Response validation)     │
│  │   └── Tools: TestContainers, Postman/Newman, REST Assured │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── End-to-End Tests                                         │
│  │   ├── Test complete user journeys (Register → Book → Pay)  │
│  │   ├── Browser automation (Cypress, Playwright, Selenium)   │
│  │   ├── Mobile app testing (Appium, Detox)                  │
│  │   └── Run in staging environment before production        │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Performance Tests                                        │
│  │   ├── Load testing (Expected concurrent users)             │
│  │   ├── Stress testing (Beyond expected load)               │
│  │   ├── Soak testing (Sustained load over time)             │
│  │   └── Tools: JMeter, Gatling, k6, Locust                  │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Security Tests                                          │
│      ├── OWASP ZAP / Burp Suite scans                         │
│      ├── Dependency vulnerability scans (OWASP Dependency-Check)│
│      ├── Penetration testing (External firm quarterly)         │
│      └── Secrets scanning (No hardcoded keys/tokens in code)  │
│                                                             │
│  DOCUMENTATION STANDARDS:                                    │
│  ├── API Documentation (Auto-generated from code)              │
│  │   ├── OpenAPI/Swagger specification                      │
│  │   ├── Examples for every endpoint                        │
│  │   ├── Error response examples                            │
│  │   └── Authentication instructions                          │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Architecture Decision Records (ADRs)                   │
│  │   ├── Context: Decision context and problem statement    │
│  │   ├── Options considered and trade-offs                 │
│  │   ├── Decision made and rationale                       │
│  │   ├── Consequences (Positive and negative)                │
│  │   └── Store in version control alongside code             │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── README Files                                            │
│  │   ├── Project README (Setup, run, deploy instructions)   │
│  │   ├── Module README (Purpose, dependencies, how to use)  │
│  │   ├── API README (How to integrate)                      │
│  │   └── CONTRIBUTING.md (For open source / team guidelines)│
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Change Log (CHANGES.md or RELEASE_NOTES.md)             │
│      ├── Version-by-version feature list                    │
│      ├── Breaking changes highlighted                        │
│      ├── Migration guides for major versions                │
│      └── Known issues and workarounds                       │
│                                                             │
│  CI/CD PIPELINE STANDARDS:                                    │
│  ├── Trigger: On every Pull Request to develop/main          │
│  ├── Stages:                                                 │
│  │   1. Build: Compile code, check syntax errors             │
│  │   2. Unit Test: Run unit tests, enforce coverage gate     │
│  │   3. Integration Test: Run integration tests             │
│  │   4. Code Quality: SonarQube analysis (Quality gate)      │
│  │   5. Security Scan: Dependency check, SAST              │
│  │   6. Build Docker Image: Containerize application           │
│  │   7. Deploy to Dev/Staging: Automated deployment          │
│  │   8. Smoke Test: Basic health check on deployed env       │
│  │   └── Notify: Success/failure notification to team         │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Quality Gates (Block promotion if failed):               │
│  │   ├── Unit test coverage < 80% → BLOCK                    │
│  │   ├── New code smells detected → BLOCK                    │
│  │   ├── Critical vulnerabilities → BLOCK                   │
│  │   ├── Broken tests → BLOCK                                │
│  │   └── Documentation missing for public APIs → WARN        │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Environments:                                           │
│  │   ├── Development: Latest code, per-developer instances   │
│  │   ├── Staging: Production-like config, integrated testing  │
│  │   ├── UAT: User acceptance testing environment           │
│  │   ├── Production: Live environment (Restricted access)    │
│  │   └── DR: Disaster recovery site (Mirror of prod)        │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Deployment Strategy                                     │
│      ├── Blue-Green Deployment (Zero-downtime deployments)      │
│      ├── Canary Releases (Roll out to 5% users first)          │
│      ├── Feature Flags (Toggle features without deployment)    │
│      ├── Rollback capability (Instant revert if issues)        │
│      └── Database migrations handled separately (Run before   │
│          app deployment)                                       │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### **7. DevOps & Operations Principles**

```txt
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              DEVOPS & OPERATIONS STANDARDS                   │
│                                                             │
│  INFRASTRUCTURE AS CODE (IaC):                               │
│  ├── All infrastructure defined in code (Terraform/CloudFormation│
│  │   /Pulumi)                                               │
│  ├── Version controlled like application code                 │
│  ├── Environment parity (Dev = Staging = Prod configuration)     │
│  └── Destroy and recreate environments easily                 │
│                                                             │
│  CONTAINERIZATION:                                           │
│  ├── Docker containers for all services                       │
│  ├── Kubernetes for orchestration (Production)                 │
│  ├── Docker Compose for development/local setup               │
│  ├── Immutable images (Rebuild, don't update running container)│
│  └── Resource limits (CPU/Memory requests and limits)          │
│                                                             │
│  OBSERVABILITY:                                             │
│  ├── Three Pillars of Observability:                       │
│  │   ├── Logs: Structured, centralized, searchable           │
│  │   │   Format: JSON, Fields: timestamp, level, service,    │
│  │   │   message, traceId, userId                              │
│  │   ├── Metrics: Quantitative measurements                   │
│  │   │   RED method: Rate (Requests/sec), Errors (%),         │
│  │   │   Duration (Latency p50/p95/p99)                     │
│  │   │   Business metrics: Registrations/hour, Revenue/day     │
│  │   └── Tracing: Request journey across services             │
│  │       Distributed tracing (Jaeger, Zipkin, AWS X-Ray)       │
│  │       Correlation IDs passed through all calls             │
│  │                                                               │
│  ├── Centralized Logging: ELK Stack (Elasticsearch, Logstash,  │
│  │   Kibana) or CloudWatch Logs / Splunk                     │
│  │                                                               │
│  ├── Metrics: Prometheus + Grafana                            │
│  │   ├── System metrics: CPU, Memory, Disk, Network          │
│  │   ├── Application metrics: Custom business KPIs            │
│  │   ├── Dashboard: Real-time operational visibility          │
│  │   └── Alerting rules (Uptime, latency, error rate)        │
│  │                                                               │
│  └── Uptime Monitoring: UptimeRobot, Pingdom, Statuspage       │
│      Public status page for users to check system health       │
│                                                             │
│  HIGH AVAILABILITY TARGETS:                                   │
│  ├── Target: 99.9% uptime (8.76 hours downtime/year max)      │
│  │   (99.99% = 52.56 minutes/year, 99.999% = 5.26 minutes)  │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Redundancy: No single point of failure                  │
│  │   ├── Application: Multi-instance behind load balancer     │
│  │   ├── Database: Primary + Replica(s) + Automated failover   │
│  │   ├── Storage: RAID, distributed storage, backups         │
│  │   ├── Network: Multiple ISPs, redundant routers             │
│  │   └── Power: UPS + Generator for critical systems          │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Disaster Recovery:                                     │
│  │   ├── RPO (Recovery Point Objective): < 1 hour data loss   │
│  │   ├── RTO (Recovery Time Objective): < 4 hours to restore   │
│  │   ├── Backups:                                             │
│  │   │   ├── Full backup: Daily (Off-peak hours)              │
│  │   │   ├── Incremental: Every 4-6 hours                   │
│  │   │   ├── Transaction log: Continuous (WAL)               │
│  │   │   └── Backup retention: 90 days on-site, 7 years off-  │
│  │   │       site (or per regulatory requirement)               │
│  │   ├── DR Site: Geographically separated secondary data center│
│  │   │   ├── Active-Passive or Active-Active replication     │
│  │   │   └── Tested failover drills (Quarterly minimum)        │
│  │   └── Runbook: Documented DR procedures, contact tree       │
│  │                                                               │
│  └── Incident Management:                                    │
│      ├── Severity levels: SEV1 (Critical, entire system down)    │
│      │                SEV2 (Major feature broken)                │
│      │                SEV3 (Minor feature degraded)              │
│      │                SEV4 (Cosmetic issue)                     │
│      ├── Response SLAs:                                       │
│      │   SEV1: 15 min response, 1 hour resolution target      │
│      │   SEV2: 30 min response, 4 hour resolution target      │
│      │   SEV3: 2 hour response, 24 hour resolution target      │
│      │   SEV4: 1 day response, 1 week resolution target       │
│      ├── Post-incident review (Root cause, prevention)          │
│      └── War room coordination during major incidents            │
│                                                             │
│  BACKUP STRATEGY:                                            │
│  ├── Database Backups                                        │
│  │   ├── Logical backups (SQL dumps/exports) - Quick restore    │
│  │   ├── Physical backups (Binary copy of data files)         │
│  │   ├── Encrypted backups (Encryption at rest)               │
│  │   └── Offsite copies (Cloud storage / Tape library)        │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Application Backups                                     │
│  │   ├── Configuration files version controlled               │
│  │   ├── Media/uploads backed up to object storage            │
│  │   └── Document templates backed up                         │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Backup Verification                                      │
│      ├── Restore testing (Monthly: Random restore to test env)  │
│      ├── Backup integrity checks (Checksum verification)         │
│      └── Recovery runbook tested and updated                  │
│                                                             │
│  SECURITY OPERATIONS:                                        │
│  ├── Patch Management                                       │
│  │   ├── OS patches: Within 30 days of release               │
│  │   ├── Application dependencies: Weekly vulnerability scan    │
│  │   └── Third-party libraries: Monthly update cycle          │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Secret Management                                      │
│  │   ├── No secrets in code (Use vault/secrets manager)       │
│  │   ├── Environment variables for configs (Not in source)    │
│  │   ├── Rotation schedule: Every 90 days for all secrets     │
│  │   └── Different secrets per environment (Dev ≠ Prod keys)  │
│  │                                                           │
│  ├── Access Control                                         │
│  │   ├── VPN required for production access                 │
│  │   ├── Bastion host/jump server (No direct SSH to servers) │
│  │   ├── Just-in-time access (Temporary elevated privs)       │
│  │   ├── Session recording for privileged access            │
│  │   └── Access reviews (Quarterly entitlement certification) │
│  │                                                           │
│  └── Vulnerability Management                                │
│      ├── Weekly automated vulnerability scanning                │
│      ├── Monthly penetration testing (By external red team)    │
│      ├── Bug bounty program (If applicable)                   │
│      ├── CVE tracking for all components                    │
│      └── Patch deployment within agreed SLA                  │
│                                                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```
